### WHERE and WHICH: Iterative Debate for Biomedical Synthetic Data Augmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23673v1)
- **Authors**: Zhengyi Zhao, Shubo Zhang, Bin Liang, Binyang Li, Kam-Fai Wong
- **Abstract**: In Biomedical Natural Language Processing (BioNLP) tasks, such as Relation Extraction, Named Entity Recognition, and Text Classification, the scarcity of high-quality data remains a significant challenge. This limitation poisons large language models to correctly understand relationships between biological entities, such as molecules and diseases, or drug interactions, and further results in potential misinterpretation of biomedical documents. To address this issue, current approaches generally adopt the Synthetic Data Augmentation method which involves similarity computation followed by word replacement, but counterfactual data are usually generated. As a result, these methods disrupt meaningful word sets or produce sentences with meanings that deviate substantially from the original context, rendering them ineffective in improving model performance. To this end, this paper proposes a biomedical-dedicated rationale-based synthetic data augmentation method. Beyond the naive lexicon similarity, specific bio-relation similarity is measured to hold the augmented instance having a strong correlation with bio-relation instead of simply increasing the diversity of augmented data. Moreover, a multi-agents-involved reflection mechanism helps the model iteratively distinguish different usage of similar entities to escape falling into the mis-replace trap. We evaluate our method on the BLURB and BigBIO benchmark, which includes 9 common datasets spanning four major BioNLP tasks. Our experimental results demonstrate consistent performance improvements across all tasks, highlighting the effectiveness of our approach in addressing the challenges associated with data scarcity and enhancing the overall performance of biomedical NLP models.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper "WHERE and WHICH: Iterative Debate for Biomedical Synthetic Data Augmentation." Here's a summary, followed by a critical evaluation and a score.

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the problem of data scarcity in biomedical natural language processing (BioNLP) tasks, where high-quality annotated data is limited. The authors propose a novel synthetic data augmentation (SDA) method called BioRDA (Biomedical-dedicated Rationale-based Data Augmentation) to generate more training data.  BioRDA goes beyond naive lexicon similarity and word replacement by:

1.  **Position Selection ("WHERE"):**  It measures bio-relation similarity to ensure augmented instances maintain a strong correlation with the original bio-relation. It utilizes a novel similarity computation approach that considers token-entity similarity, token-entity tuple similarity, and bio-relation context.
2.  **Word Selection ("WHICH"):** It employs a multi-agent "Advise-Reflect-Revise" paradigm, where agents iteratively debate the appropriateness of word replacements based on factors like word definitions, syntax correctness, and usage examples, preventing mis-replacements and logical errors.

The authors evaluate BioRDA on nine datasets spanning four BioNLP tasks from the BLURB and BigBIO benchmarks.  Experimental results demonstrate consistent performance improvements compared to baseline SDA methods and even some task-specific models, highlighting BioRDA's effectiveness in addressing data scarcity challenges in BioNLP.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper introduces a few novel components:

    *   The explicit separation of the SDA process into "WHERE" (position selection) and "WHICH" (word selection) phases offers a more structured and controlled approach compared to existing methods.
    *   The bio-relation similarity metric seems to be a good way to measure, beyond standard cosine similarity, if a given potential alteration of the sentence would still preserve the underlying relation.
    *   The multi-agent debate system is an interesting approach to ensure the coherence and validity of the generated synthetic data, moving beyond simple word replacements. It mimics a human review process to catch errors and nuances.

*   **Significance:**

    *   Data scarcity is a persistent problem in BioNLP, making SDA a valuable research area.  The paper's improvement over strong baselines, including other SDA techniques and NLI transfer methods, demonstrates the potential of BioRDA.
    *   The performance gains on established benchmarks (BLURB and BigBIO) strengthen the claim that BioRDA is beneficial for a range of BioNLP tasks.
    *   The paper does a good job of ablating the importance of the different parts of the framework. Specifically that it is worthwhile to consider where the replacement can affect syntax (the WHERE portion) and WHICH word should be selected (the WHICH portion)

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the proposed method and experimental setup.
    *   The evaluation is thorough, covering multiple datasets and tasks.
    *   The results are convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of BioRDA.
    *   The ablation study provides insights into the contribution of different components.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on large language models (LLMs) for both the agents in the debate system and in building the syntactic re-fix module, while effective, raises questions about computational cost and accessibility.  The paper could benefit from a discussion of resource requirements and potential optimizations.
    *   Although the work uses LLMs and other high performance models, the model itself is not explicitly described. More details on how all components are implemented in this paper is needed.
    *   The multi-agent system is interesting, but the rules and logic of each agent should be presented in great details to reproduce the work.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The proposed "WHERE-WHICH" framework could influence future SDA research by promoting a more structured and rationale-based approach.
    *   The multi-agent debate system could be adapted for other NLP tasks where data quality and coherence are crucial.
    *   The paper might encourage further exploration of combining semantic and syntactic information for improved SDA in specialized domains like biomedicine.

**Score: 7.8**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of BioNLP by introducing a novel and effective SDA method. The "WHERE-WHICH" framework and multi-agent debate system represent a step forward compared to traditional SDA techniques. While the reliance on LLMs poses a practical limitation, the performance gains on established benchmarks and the well-designed evaluation demonstrate the potential of BioRDA.  The paper is well-written and clearly presents its contributions. Further explanation of the different components implementation details could improve reproducibility, but still the work is good. Therefore, a score of 7.8 reflects the paper's notable novelty, significant results, and potential influence on future research in data augmentation for biomedical NLP.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models Pass the Turing Test
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23674v1)
- **Authors**: Cameron R. Jones, Benjamin K. Bergen
- **Abstract**: We evaluated 4 systems (ELIZA, GPT-4o, LLaMa-3.1-405B, and GPT-4.5) in two randomised, controlled, and pre-registered Turing tests on independent populations. Participants had 5 minute conversations simultaneously with another human participant and one of these systems before judging which conversational partner they thought was human. When prompted to adopt a humanlike persona, GPT-4.5 was judged to be the human 73% of the time: significantly more often than interrogators selected the real human participant. LLaMa-3.1, with the same prompt, was judged to be the human 56% of the time -- not significantly more or less often than the humans they were being compared to -- while baseline models (ELIZA and GPT-4o) achieved win rates significantly below chance (23% and 21% respectively). The results constitute the first empirical evidence that any artificial system passes a standard three-party Turing test. The results have implications for debates about what kind of intelligence is exhibited by Large Language Models (LLMs), and the social and economic impacts these systems are likely to have.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents the first empirical evidence that a large language model (LLM), specifically GPT-4.5, can pass a standard three-party Turing test.  The authors conducted two randomized, controlled, and pre-registered Turing tests where human interrogators had conversations with both a human and one of several AI systems (GPT-4.5, LLaMa-3.1-405B, GPT-40, and ELIZA).  The interrogators then judged which participant they believed was human.  When prompted to adopt a humanlike persona, GPT-4.5 was identified as human significantly more often than the real human participant, indicating it "passed" the Turing test.  LLaMa-3.1, with the same prompt, was judged human at a rate not significantly different from chance, while baseline models (ELIZA and GPT-40) performed significantly below chance.  The study was conducted with two independent populations (undergraduates and Prolific workers) to enhance robustness. The paper also includes analysis of the strategies used by interrogators and the reasons for their judgements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper provides significant novelty.  While previous work explored LLMs in Turing-like tests, this paper is the first to provide strong evidence that an LLM can outperform humans in the classic three-party setting. The use of a pre-registered design, a control group (ELIZA), and replication across different populations adds to the rigor of the study. The inclusion of a second high performing LLM (LLaMA) provides a more nuanced picture of the performance of contemporary AI systems in this test. The analysis of strategies and reasons offers insights into how humans perceive and evaluate AI systems in conversation.

*   **Significance:** The paper has substantial significance, impacting debates about AI intelligence and societal implications.  The finding that an AI system can successfully imitate human conversation to the point of deceiving humans has important implications for AI ethics, job automation, and social engineering. It challenges existing metrics for evaluating AI and highlights the importance of interactive, adversarial tests like the Turing test. Further, the analysis of interrogator strategies sheds light on the kind of human behaviour that LLMs are (or are not) successfully simulating, and which features humans rely on when assessing 'humanness' in conversation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous methodology:** Pre-registration, randomization, control conditions, and replication.
    *   **Well-defined experimental setup:**  Clear description of the AI models, prompts, and experimental procedure.
    *   **Analysis of results:** The use of win rates, confidence intervals, and analyses to test pre-registered hypotheses.
    *   **Meaningful controls**: The ELIZA baseline model provides an important basis for comparison with the experimental models, and also highlights the need for better testing frameworks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Prompt dependence:** GPT-4.5 only "passed" the Turing test with a specific persona prompt. This raises questions about how much of the success is attributable to the model vs. the prompt engineering. (The authors acknowledge and address this point, arguing it's a distinction without a difference.)
    *   **Conversation length:** The 5-minute conversations might not be long enough to expose all the limitations of the AI systems.
    *   **Population:** While two populations were used, they are not representative of all people and results might vary across different demographics and cultures.
    *   **Limited number of AI systems tested:** While the paper evaluated multiple AI systems, the results may not generalize to all LLMs.
    *   **Strategy Analysis:** Although the paper provided a detailed analysis of strategies and reasons. These could be improved by incorporating the full conversational context in the models.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work is likely to be highly influential because it is the first to definitively show that an AI system can "pass" the Turing test in its classic formulation. This will spur further research on: 1) what it means for AI systems to pass such tests, 2) how AI systems can be made more transparent and trustworthy, 3) the broader societal implications of increasingly human-like AI.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** While some argue that the Turing Test is an outdated or problematic measure of intelligence, the paper's significance lies in demonstrating that *deception* is now within the reach of existing AI. This deception has real world consequences in social, political, and economic contexts. The authors have also responded appropriately to several criticisms of the Turing test by incorporating the most well-regarded interpretations, and also responding to various issues including prompt engineering, conversation length, and population diversity.

**Score: 8**

The paper is novel, well-executed, and of high significance, making a substantial contribution to the field and prompting important future research directions. Its primary weakness stems from the prompt dependence of the results. Nonetheless, the strong methodology and clear analysis justify a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Mapping Geopolitical Bias in 11 Large Language Models: A Bilingual, Dual-Framing Analysis of U.S.-China Tensions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23688v1)
- **Authors**: William Guey, Pierrick Bougault, Vitor D. de Moura, Wei Zhang, Jose O. Gomes
- **Abstract**: This study systematically analyzes geopolitical bias across 11 prominent Large Language Models (LLMs) by examining their responses to seven critical topics in U.S.-China relations. Utilizing a bilingual (English and Chinese) and dual-framing (affirmative and reverse) methodology, we generated 19,712 prompts designed to detect ideological leanings in model outputs. Responses were quantitatively assessed on a normalized scale from -2 (strongly Pro-China) to +2 (strongly Pro-U.S.) and categorized according to stance, neutrality, and refusal rates. The findings demonstrate significant and consistent ideological alignments correlated with the LLMs' geographic origins; U.S.-based models predominantly favored Pro-U.S. stances, while Chinese-origin models exhibited pronounced Pro-China biases. Notably, language and prompt framing substantially influenced model responses, with several LLMs exhibiting stance reversals based on prompt polarity or linguistic context. Additionally, we introduced comprehensive metrics to evaluate response consistency across languages and framing conditions, identifying variability and vulnerabilities in model behaviors. These results offer practical insights that can guide organizations and individuals in selecting LLMs best aligned with their operational priorities and geopolitical considerations, underscoring the importance of careful model evaluation in politically sensitive applications. Furthermore, the research highlights specific prompt structures and linguistic variations that can strategically trigger distinct responses from models, revealing methods for effectively navigating and influencing LLM outputs.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided paper abstract and information to generate a summary and critical evaluation.

**Summary:**

The paper "Mapping Geopolitical Bias in 11 Large Language Models: A Bilingual, Dual-Framing Analysis of U.S.–China Tensions" systematically investigates geopolitical biases in 11 large language models (LLMs) concerning U.S.-China relations. It employs a bilingual (English and Chinese) and dual-framing (affirmative and reversed prompts) methodology to generate and analyze 19,712 prompts. The LLMs' responses were quantitatively assessed and categorized. The study reveals significant ideological alignments correlated with the LLMs' geographic origins, U.S.-based models favoring Pro-U.S. stances, and Chinese-origin models exhibiting Pro-China biases. The research highlights the impact of language and prompt framing on model responses and introduces metrics to evaluate response consistency. The findings provide practical insights for selecting LLMs in politically sensitive applications and identify strategies to influence LLM outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper demonstrates several aspects of novelty. The systematic examination of geopolitical bias in LLMs is relatively new, and this study goes beyond existing work by:

*   **Bilingual Approach:** Incorporating Chinese language prompts alongside English is a valuable addition, as it explores potential cultural and linguistic influences on bias.
*   **Dual-Framing Methodology:** Using affirmative and reversed prompts allows for a more robust assessment of ideological consistency and framing sensitivity, directly addressing concerns about prompt manipulation.
*   **Quantitative Assessment:**  The approach of quantifying and categorizing model responses on a normalized scale provides a more rigorous and comparable measure than purely qualitative analyses.
*   **Introducing Consistency Metrics**: The addition of neutrality and refusal rates alongside preference scores offer valuable context in analyzing model behaviors.

However, it's important to acknowledge that other recent papers also tackle similar aspects (as noted in the reference to Pacheco et al. and Rozado's work on general political orientations). The novelty primarily lies in the combination of these elements and the focus on a specific, sensitive geopolitical relationship (U.S.-China).

**Significance:** The research has significant implications for:

*   **Understanding LLM Biases:** The findings contribute to a growing body of knowledge about the biases embedded in LLMs, emphasizing the importance of careful evaluation before deployment.
*   **Informing Model Selection:** The paper provides practical guidance for organizations and individuals in selecting LLMs aligned with their operational priorities and geopolitical considerations. This is particularly valuable in sectors where neutrality or specific viewpoints are crucial (e.g., news, policy advising).
*   **Highlighting Vulnerabilities:** The research identifies vulnerabilities in model alignment strategies, demonstrating how prompt structure and linguistic variations can influence responses. This has implications for model security and robustness.
*   **Guiding Future Research:** The study's methodology and findings can inform future research on bias detection and mitigation in LLMs, particularly in politically sensitive contexts.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Topic Selection:** While the focus on U.S.-China relations is relevant, the findings may not generalize to other geopolitical relationships or political issues. The chosen topics are inherently limited.
*   **Model Selection:**  The choice of LLMs is appropriate, but it's crucial to note that the field is rapidly evolving. The models tested might become outdated relatively quickly.  The static analysis limits the applicability of the results over a long period, as models continue to evolve and update.
*   **Inference vs. Causation:**  The study identifies correlations between model origin and bias, but it cannot establish causation. Other factors, such as training data composition and fine-tuning strategies, may also play a role.
*   **Quantifiable Bias:** The paper utilizes a quantifiable approach which gives the impression of impartiality. However, there are many challenges involved in precisely measuring ideological bias in LLMs. The assignment of scores may require further elaboration.

**Justification:**

The paper offers a substantial and timely contribution to the understanding of geopolitical biases in LLMs. It builds upon existing work by incorporating a bilingual and dual-framing methodology, providing a more rigorous and nuanced assessment than previous studies. The practical implications for model selection and vulnerability identification are significant. While the study has limitations, such as its focus on a specific geopolitical relationship and the dynamic nature of the field, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses.

Therefore,

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Conceptual Framework for Human-AI Collaborative Genome Annotation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23691v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaomei Li, Alex Whan, Meredith McNeil, David Starns, Jessica Irons, Samuel C. Andrew, Rad Suchecki
- **Abstract**: Genome annotation is essential for understanding the functional elements within genomes. While automated methods are indispensable for processing large-scale genomic data, they often face challenges in accurately predicting gene structures and functions. Consequently, manual curation by domain experts remains crucial for validating and refining these predictions. These combined outcomes from automated tools and manual curation highlight the importance of integrating human expertise with AI capabilities to improve both the accuracy and efficiency of genome annotation. However, the manual curation process is inherently labor-intensive and time-consuming, making it difficult to scale for large datasets. To address these challenges, we propose a conceptual framework, Human-AI Collaborative Genome Annotation (HAICoGA), which leverages the synergistic partnership between humans and artificial intelligence to enhance human capabilities and accelerate the genome annotation process. Additionally, we explore the potential of integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) into this framework to support and augment specific tasks. Finally, we discuss emerging challenges and outline open research questions to guide further exploration in this area.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a conceptual framework called HAICOGA (Human-AI Collaborative Genome Annotation) for genome annotation. It addresses the limitations of purely automated and purely manual annotation approaches by advocating for a synergistic collaboration between humans and AI. The paper identifies key components of the framework: humans, AI systems and tools, data, goals and tasks, human-machine interface, environment, and collaboration. It explores the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) in this framework, and discusses challenges related to its implementation, including the design of multi-agent systems, and the development of appropriate evaluation methods.  The paper concludes by outlining future research directions aimed at realizing HAICOGA.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of a collaborative framework for genome annotation, leveraging the strengths of both humans and AI, is valuable but not entirely novel. The paper builds on existing work in human-AI collaboration and applies it to a specific domain.  While the "HAICOGA" framework and the exploration of LLMs in this context are contributions, the fundamental concept of human-in-the-loop or collaborative AI is well-established. The novelty lies more in the specific instantiation of this idea for genome annotation and the consideration of LLMs.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in genomics: the accuracy and efficiency of genome annotation. By proposing HAICOGA, the authors offer a potential solution to improve both. If successfully implemented, HAICOGA could accelerate research, improve our understanding of gene function, and ultimately lead to downstream applications.  The paper provides a structured framework for approaching the problem, which is useful to the community.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing genome annotation methods.
    * **Comprehensive Framework:** The HAICOGA framework provides a structured way to think about human-AI collaboration in this context.  The identified components are relevant and well-defined.
    * **LLM Integration:**  The paper appropriately highlights the potential of LLMs to enhance the framework.
    * **Discussion of Challenges:** The authors acknowledge the challenges involved in implementing HAICOGA, which strengthens the paper.
    * **Extensive Literature Review:**  The paper has a strong literature review, drawing from various relevant fields including human-computer interaction and artificial intelligence.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Lack of Empirical Validation:** The paper is entirely conceptual. There's no implementation or evaluation of the HAICOGA framework, even in a preliminary form. This limits the paper's impact.
    * **Limited Depth on Technical Aspects:** While the paper introduces the concepts, it lacks detail on the specific AI/ML techniques or algorithms that would be employed within the framework.  How would the various agents interact?  How would the LLM be fine-tuned for genome annotation tasks?
    * **The framework is quite general and could benefit from more specifics related to genome annotation. This includes detailing specific genomic features, tools, and challenges that are more specific to genome annotation**.

* **Potential Impact:**  The paper has the potential to influence research in human-AI collaboration for genome annotation. By providing a conceptual framework, it can guide the development of new tools and workflows. However, the actual impact depends on whether researchers build upon HAICOGA and validate its effectiveness in practice.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper offers a useful conceptual framework for human-AI collaboration in genome annotation, integrating the potential of LLMs and outlining critical design elements. However, the complete absence of empirical validation significantly lowers its score. The framework is also somewhat high-level, and lacks sufficient detail on the technical implementation aspects. The paper also lacks a clear, focused hypothesis and concrete objectives to drive future research.

Score: 5

- **Score**: 5/10

### Expanding-and-Shrinking Binary Neural Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23709v1)
- **Authors**: Xulong Shi, Caiyi Sun, Zhi Qi, Liu Hao, Xiaodong Yang
- **Abstract**: While binary neural networks (BNNs) offer significant benefits in terms of speed, memory and energy, they encounter substantial accuracy degradation in challenging tasks compared to their real-valued counterparts. Due to the binarization of weights and activations, the possible values of each entry in the feature maps generated by BNNs are strongly constrained. To tackle this limitation, we propose the expanding-and-shrinking operation, which enhances binary feature maps with negligible increase of computation complexity, thereby strengthening the representation capacity. Extensive experiments conducted on multiple benchmarks reveal that our approach generalizes well across diverse applications ranging from image classification, object detection to generative diffusion model, while also achieving remarkable improvement over various leading binarization algorithms based on different architectures including both CNNs and Transformers.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Expanding-and-Shrinking Binary Neural Networks" (ES-BNN), a technique designed to improve the accuracy of Binary Neural Networks (BNNs) without significantly increasing computational complexity. BNNs offer advantages in speed, memory, and energy efficiency, but often suffer from accuracy degradation compared to real-valued networks due to the binarization of weights and activations.  ES-BNN addresses this by expanding the input channels and shrinking the convolution kernels of each layer, thereby enhancing the representation capacity of the output features.  The paper combines this operation with binary group convolution to improve the balance between expanding and shrinking. They demonstrate the effectiveness of ES-BNN across various tasks (image classification, object detection, super-resolution) and network architectures (CNNs, Transformers).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of expanding and shrinking channels in neural networks isn't entirely new. Prior work has explored channel expansion/reduction techniques in different contexts, often related to pruning or efficient network design. However, the specific application of this idea *within the context of BNNs* to improve representation capacity while *explicitly maintaining computation complexity* is the paper's primary novel contribution. The integration with binary group convolution to balance expansion/shrinkage further refines the idea. The key here is tailoring this technique to address the unique limitations of BNNs.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the persistent challenge of improving BNN accuracy. BNNs offer compelling resource advantages, making them highly desirable for deployment on resource-constrained devices. Therefore, any method that substantially boosts accuracy without undermining these advantages is valuable. The experimental results showing consistent improvements across diverse tasks and architectures are compelling. The fact that it works with existing binarization algorithms makes it more readily adoptable. While other techniques (e.g. multi-bit quantization) can boost BNN accuracy, they often come at the cost of increased complexity or memory.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the issue of limited representation capacity in BNNs and positions its approach as a solution to this fundamental problem.
    *   **Simplicity and Generalizability:** The expanding-and-shrinking operation itself is relatively simple to implement and integrate into existing BNN frameworks.  The experiments support claims of generalizability across tasks, architectures, and binarization algorithms.
    *   **Thorough Experimental Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments on multiple datasets and tasks, including ablation studies and comparisons to state-of-the-art methods.
    *   **Computational Complexity Analysis:** The paper provides a detailed analysis of the computational complexity, demonstrating that ES-BNN introduces minimal overhead.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** Visualization techniques (t-SNE and loss landscapes) offer insights into how ES-BNN improves feature representation and optimization.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the paper provides an intuitive explanation of how ES-BNN enhances representation capacity, a more rigorous theoretical analysis would strengthen the claims.  For example, connecting the expanded feature space to concepts like VC dimension or expressivity could add depth.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** Although the general approach is sound, the hyper-parameters *T* and *g* likely require careful tuning for optimal performance on different datasets/architectures. The paper addresses this via experiments, but a more adaptive or automated selection method could enhance practicality.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** It is essentially an engineering contribution improving existing BNNs. However, it would have benefited from a theoretical analysis for insights into why it works well.
    *   **Limited Comparison with Multi-Bit Quantization:** While the paper acknowledges alternative quantization techniques, a more direct comparison (e.g., in terms of accuracy, memory footprint, and energy consumption) would provide a clearer picture of the tradeoffs involved.

*   **Potential Influence:** ES-BNN has the potential to be a practical and widely applicable technique for improving the performance of BNNs in various applications. Its simplicity and generalizability make it an attractive option for researchers and practitioners working with resource-constrained devices.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable engineering contribution that addresses a significant challenge in the field of BNNs. The expanding-and-shrinking operation is a simple yet effective technique that demonstrably improves accuracy without sacrificing the computational advantages of BNNs. The extensive experimental evaluation and analysis support the claims of generalizability and efficiency. While the paper lacks a deeper theoretical analysis and a more direct comparison with other quantization methods, its practical impact and potential for widespread adoption justify a score of 7.5. The primary strengths reside in its simplicity, demonstrated effectiveness, and the potential for readily enhancing existing BNN frameworks.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Building Instruction-Tuning Datasets from Human-Written Instructions with Open-Weight Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23714v1)
- **Authors**: Youmi Ma, Sakae Mizuki, Kazuki Fujii, Taishi Nakamura, Masanari Ohi, Hinari Shimada, Taihei Shiotani, Koshiro Saito, Koki Maeda, Kakeru Hattori, Takumi Okamoto, Shigeki Ishida, Rio Yokota, Hiroya Takamura, Naoaki Okazaki
- **Abstract**: Instruction tuning is crucial for enabling Large Language Models (LLMs) to solve real-world tasks. Prior work has shown the effectiveness of instruction-tuning data synthesized solely from LLMs, raising a fundamental question: Do we still need human-originated signals for instruction tuning? This work answers the question affirmatively: we build state-of-the-art instruction-tuning datasets sourced from human-written instructions, by simply pairing them with LLM-generated responses. LLMs fine-tuned on our datasets consistently outperform those fine-tuned on existing ones. Our data construction approach can be easily adapted to other languages; we build datasets for Japanese and confirm that LLMs tuned with our data reach state-of-the-art performance. Analyses suggest that instruction-tuning in a new language allows LLMs to follow instructions, while the tuned models exhibit a notable lack of culture-specific knowledge in that language. The datasets and fine-tuned models will be publicly available. Our datasets, synthesized with open-weight LLMs, are openly distributed under permissive licenses, allowing for diverse use cases.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the question of whether human-written instructions are still necessary for instruction tuning Large Language Models (LLMs), given the recent success of self-synthesized data. The authors create new instruction-tuning datasets by pairing human-written instructions from the LMSYS-Chat-1M dataset with responses generated by open-weight LLMs (Llama-3.1 and Gemma-2). They fine-tune several LLMs on these datasets and show that they consistently outperform models fine-tuned on existing datasets, including Magpie, which uses LLM-generated instructions.  The approach is extended to Japanese using machine translation, and the resulting models also achieve state-of-the-art performance. Analysis reveals that instruction tuning with Japanese data allows LLMs to follow instructions well but doesn't necessarily improve culture-specific knowledge. The datasets and fine-tuned models are released publicly under permissive licenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a Relevant Question:** The paper tackles a fundamental and timely question about the necessity of human input in instruction tuning.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The methodology is straightforward and easy to reproduce. The authors leverage a readily available dataset of human-chatbot interactions for instructions and use powerful open-weight LLMs for response generation.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments provide compelling evidence that LLMs fine-tuned on the proposed datasets outperform those fine-tuned on existing datasets (Magpie) across several models (Qwen, Llama, Gemma, OLMo). This clearly demonstrates the value of human-written instructions.
    *   **Multi-Lingual Validation:** The extension to Japanese strengthens the paper by showing the generalizability of the approach. The analysis of cross-lingual transfer is insightful and sheds light on the different roles of instruction tuning and pre-training.
    *   **Open and Accessible Resources:** Publicly releasing the datasets and fine-tuned models is a significant contribution to the community. The permissive licenses encourage diverse use cases and further research.
    *   **Well-Written and Clear:** The paper is well-structured and easy to understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the paper presents a valuable contribution, the core idea of using human-written instructions isn't entirely novel (existing datasets use human instructions). The main novelty lies in the specific dataset construction process and its performance against recent self-synthesized methods.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Instruction Types:** The paper does not deeply analyze the characteristics of the human-written instructions that make them more effective. Further investigation into the types of instructions present in LMSYS-Chat-1M and their impact would be beneficial.
    *   **Dependence on Translation Quality:** The performance of the Japanese models is somewhat dependent on the quality of machine translation. While DeepL is a strong translator, translation errors could still affect the results.
    *   **Limited exploration of different LLM "teachers":** They used Llama-3.1 and Gemma-2 as teachers. Given the result in 4.2 (the effectiveness of model imitation for non-English languages does not necessarily align with the findings in English), using other LLMs may lead to different conclusions

*   **Significance:**

The paper's findings have important implications for the field of instruction tuning. It shows that human-written instructions, even when paired with LLM-generated responses, can still lead to state-of-the-art performance. This suggests that efforts to collect and curate high-quality human-generated instructions are still worthwhile. The publicly available datasets and models will likely be valuable resources for researchers and practitioners.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by rigorously demonstrating the continued value of human-written instructions for instruction tuning, even in the era of powerful LLMs and self-synthesized datasets. While the core idea isn't entirely novel, the empirical results, the multi-lingual validation, and the open release of resources make it a valuable contribution. The paper challenges the assumption that LLM-generated instructions are universally superior and provides a practical alternative. However, limited analysis of instruction types, dependence on translation quality and the incremental nature of the novelty keeps it from being a truly exceptional contribution.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### HOIGen-1M: A Large-scale Dataset for Human-Object Interaction Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23715v1)
- **Authors**: Kun Liu, Qi Liu, Xinchen Liu, Jie Li, Yongdong Zhang, Jiebo Luo, Xiaodong He, Wu Liu
- **Abstract**: Text-to-video (T2V) generation has made tremendous progress in generating complicated scenes based on texts. However, human-object interaction (HOI) often cannot be precisely generated by current T2V models due to the lack of large-scale videos with accurate captions for HOI. To address this issue, we introduce HOIGen-1M, the first largescale dataset for HOI Generation, consisting of over one million high-quality videos collected from diverse sources. In particular, to guarantee the high quality of videos, we first design an efficient framework to automatically curate HOI videos using the powerful multimodal large language models (MLLMs), and then the videos are further cleaned by human annotators. Moreover, to obtain accurate textual captions for HOI videos, we design a novel video description method based on a Mixture-of-Multimodal-Experts (MoME) strategy that not only generates expressive captions but also eliminates the hallucination by individual MLLM. Furthermore, due to the lack of an evaluation framework for generated HOI videos, we propose two new metrics to assess the quality of generated videos in a coarse-to-fine manner. Extensive experiments reveal that current T2V models struggle to generate high-quality HOI videos and confirm that our HOIGen-1M dataset is instrumental for improving HOI video generation. Project webpage is available at https://liuqi-creat.github.io/HOIGen.github.io.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HOIGen-1M, a large-scale dataset for human-object interaction (HOI) video generation. Addressing the lack of datasets with accurate captions for HOI videos, the authors collected over one million high-quality videos. They developed a framework using multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to curate the videos and a novel video description method based on a Mixture-of-Multimodal-Experts (MoME) strategy for accurate captioning.  To evaluate generated HOI videos, the paper proposes two new metrics, CoarseHOIScore and FineHOIScore. Experiments demonstrate that existing text-to-video (T2V) models struggle with HOI video generation, and that HOIGen-1M helps improve performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Dataset Scale and Focus:** The creation of a million-scale dataset specifically targeted at HOI video generation is a significant contribution. Previous datasets for T2V generation were either smaller, of lower quality, or not specifically focused on HOI. The scale is sufficient for training T2V models.
    *   **Data Curation Pipeline:** The pipeline for automatically curating HOI videos using MLLMs and subsequent human annotation demonstrates a practical approach to creating a high-quality dataset from a large pool of videos. The use of several evaluation components (metadata, OCR, aesthetics score, motion score, LLM, and human verification) to curate the videos is well-thought-out.
    *   **MoME Captioning Strategy:**  The proposed Mixture-of-Multimodal-Experts (MoME) captioning strategy to mitigate hallucinations in video descriptions is a novel and effective approach. The cross-verification among multiple MLLMs is a sound way to improve caption accuracy, though the novelty of the individual components is questionable.

    *   **Evaluation Metrics:**  The introduction of CoarseHOIScore and FineHOIScore, while necessary to assess HOI video quality, could be improved. The coarse metric seems basic (HOI detection), and the fine metric based on pixel distance may not truly reflect the interaction's complexity and is sensitive to error.
    *   **The paper would be strengthened if it compared against existing datasets and captioning strategies, in terms of metrics, video examples, etc. rather than simply saying it is larger and of higher quality.**

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Addressing a Gap:**  The paper directly addresses the gap in the availability of high-quality datasets for HOI video generation, which is a major bottleneck for improving T2V models' ability to generate realistic and accurate interactions.
    *   **Impact on T2V Research:** The HOIGen-1M dataset has the potential to significantly advance research in T2V generation by enabling the training and evaluation of models that can more accurately generate HOI videos.
    *   **New Evaluation Paradigm:** The proposed HOIScore metrics provide a starting point for evaluating the quality of generated HOI videos, which can encourage the development of more sophisticated evaluation methods. The metrics are not perfect, but at least offer an automatic evaluation strategy.

    *   **Limitations:** It does not compare results on existing datasets. While the scale of the dataset is larger, the paper doesn't give any comparison against existing methods.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:**  The paper is generally well-written and organized, making it easy to understand the proposed dataset, methods, and experimental results. However, more comparisons to current methods would make it stronger.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7** to this paper.

*   **Positive Aspects:** The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by introducing a large-scale, high-quality dataset specifically designed for HOI video generation. The proposed data curation pipeline and MoME captioning strategy are novel and effective approaches for addressing the challenges of creating such a dataset. The introduction of the HOIScore metrics is a step towards more targeted evaluation of HOI video generation models.

*   **Areas for Improvement:** The introduced metrics, while novel, could be better and more complex to represent HOI quality. The paper could be strengthened by a more comprehensive evaluation against prior dataset and video generation techniques.

In conclusion, while there's room for improvement with metrics, this dataset does fulfill a real gap in the field, particularly with the rising capabilities of T2V models.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Effective Cloud Removal for Remote Sensing Images by an Improved Mean-Reverting Denoising Model with Elucidated Design Space
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23717v1)
- **Authors**: Yi Liu, Wengen Li, Jihong Guan, Shuigeng Zhou, Yichao Zhang
- **Abstract**: Cloud removal (CR) remains a challenging task in remote sensing image processing. Although diffusion models (DM) exhibit strong generative capabilities, their direct applications to CR are suboptimal, as they generate cloudless images from random noise, ignoring inherent information in cloudy inputs. To overcome this drawback, we develop a new CR model EMRDM based on mean-reverting diffusion models (MRDMs) to establish a direct diffusion process between cloudy and cloudless images. Compared to current MRDMs, EMRDM offers a modular framework with updatable modules and an elucidated design space, based on a reformulated forward process and a new ordinary differential equation (ODE)-based backward process. Leveraging our framework, we redesign key MRDM modules to boost CR performance, including restructuring the denoiser via a preconditioning technique, reorganizing the training process, and improving the sampling process by introducing deterministic and stochastic samplers. To achieve multi-temporal CR, we further develop a denoising network for simultaneously denoising sequential images. Experiments on mono-temporal and multi-temporal datasets demonstrate the superior performance of EMRDM. Our code is available at https://github.com/Ly403/EMRDM.
