### AdaMMS: Model Merging for Heterogeneous Multimodal Large Language Models with Unsupervised Coefficient Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23733v1)
- **Authors**: Yiyang Du, Xiaochen Wang, Chi Chen, Jiabo Ye, Yiru Wang, Peng Li, Ming Yan, Ji Zhang, Fei Huang, Zhifang Sui, Maosong Sun, Yang Liu
- **Abstract**: Recently, model merging methods have demonstrated powerful strengths in combining abilities on various tasks from multiple Large Language Models (LLMs). While previous model merging methods mainly focus on merging homogeneous models with identical architecture, they meet challenges when dealing with Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) with inherent heterogeneous property, including differences in model architecture and the asymmetry in the parameter space. In this work, we propose AdaMMS, a novel model merging method tailored for heterogeneous MLLMs. Our method tackles the challenges in three steps: mapping, merging and searching. Specifically, we first design mapping function between models to apply model merging on MLLMs with different architecture. Then we apply linear interpolation on model weights to actively adapt the asymmetry in the heterogeneous MLLMs. Finally in the hyper-parameter searching step, we propose an unsupervised hyper-parameter selection method for model merging. As the first model merging method capable of merging heterogeneous MLLMs without labeled data, extensive experiments on various model combinations demonstrated that AdaMMS outperforms previous model merging methods on various vision-language benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AdaMMS, a novel model merging technique designed specifically for heterogeneous Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). AdaMMS tackles the challenges arising from architectural differences and parameter space asymmetries in MLLMs through a three-step process: (1) *Mapping*, which aligns parameters between models with different architectures; (2) *Merging*, employing linear interpolation with adaptive coefficients to address parameter asymmetry; and (3) *Searching*, utilizing an unsupervised hyperparameter selection method based on the insight that model performance correlates with generation consistency (response variation) even without labeled data. Experiments on various MLLM combinations demonstrate that AdaMMS outperforms existing model merging methods on vision-language benchmarks, even without requiring labeled data for tuning, which is a key advantage.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the holistic approach to merging heterogeneous MLLMs *without* relying on labeled data.  While model merging itself isn't new, and aspects of the individual components (mapping, linear interpolation, hyperparameter search) have precedents, their combination and application to the specific problem of heterogeneous MLLMs under an unsupervised constraint represent a significant contribution. Previous approaches like FuseLLM required supervised training, making AdaMMS more resource-efficient and applicable to scenarios where labeled data is scarce. The unsupervised hyperparameter selection based on response consistency is a particularly interesting and potentially broadly applicable technique. However, the architecture differences tackled appear to be variations within a transformer setting, and not drastically different architectures, which somewhat limits the scope of the approach.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the growing importance of MLLMs and the practical limitations of training or fine-tuning them from scratch. AdaMMS provides a way to combine the strengths of different MLLMs without the cost of extensive data collection and training. The method's resource efficiency is a major advantage. The experimental results appear robust, demonstrating improved performance across a range of vision-language tasks. The "no labeled data" requirement is a strong selling point.  However, the paper would be strengthened by a more thorough error analysis, explaining *why* AdaMMS works, and where it struggles. Are there specific types of tasks, model architectures, or merging scenarios where it is less effective? Furthermore, a more extensive comparison to FuseLLM, specifically in cases where supervised training *is* feasible, would provide a clearer understanding of the trade-offs. The current analysis heavily focuses on computational efficiency, but a direct comparison in terms of peak performance achievable with supervised methods is missing. It also remains to be seen how well the generation consistency insight generalizes to other types of models and tasks. Furthermore, the model architecture modifications tackled seem limited, and the method's adaptability to more significant architecture variations should be addressed.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a relevant problem in MLLM development.
    *   Offers a practical and efficient solution (no labeled data required).
    *   Demonstrates strong empirical results across a variety of benchmarks.
    *   The unsupervised hyperparameter selection based on generation consistency is a novel idea.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The scope of architecture differences addressed is somewhat limited.
    *   The analysis lacks a thorough examination of the method's failure cases.
    *   The performance comparison with supervised methods (FuseLLM) isn't comprehensive.
    *   Generational consistency insight should be further evaluated on wider spectrum
    *   Lack of theoretical and empirical analysis of the limitations with larger architecture gaps

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of MLLMs by providing an unsupervised model merging technique that effectively combines the capabilities of heterogeneous models. The work is well-motivated, and the proposed AdaMMS method demonstrates promising results.  The unsupervised hyperparameter selection technique is a particularly interesting innovation. However, the limitations in the scope of architecture variation and the less extensive performance comparisons with supervised alternatives hold it back from a higher score. The paper's strengths significantly outweigh its weaknesses, making it a solid contribution to MLLM research and development, but there's room for improvement in terms of both theoretical and empirical validation across a wider variety of scenarios and larger architectural disparities.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LANID: LLM-assisted New Intent Discovery
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23740v1)
- **Authors**: Lu Fan, Jiashu Pu, Rongsheng Zhang, Xiao-Ming Wu
- **Abstract**: Task-oriented Dialogue Systems (TODS) often face the challenge of encountering new intents. New Intent Discovery (NID) is a crucial task that aims to identify these novel intents while maintaining the capability to recognize existing ones. Previous efforts to adapt TODS to new intents have struggled with inadequate semantic representation or have depended on external knowledge, which is often not scalable or flexible. Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong zero-shot capabilities; however, their scale can be impractical for real-world applications that involve extensive queries. To address the limitations of existing NID methods by leveraging LLMs, we propose LANID, a framework that enhances the semantic representation of lightweight NID encoders with the guidance of LLMs. Specifically, LANID employs the $K$-nearest neighbors and Density-Based Spatial Clustering of Applications with Noise (DBSCAN) algorithms to sample selective utterance pairs from the training set. It then queries an LLM to ascertain the relationships between these pairs. The data produced from this process is utilized to design a contrastive fine-tuning task, which is then used to train a small encoder with a contrastive triplet loss. Our experimental results demonstrate the efficacy of the proposed method across three distinct NID datasets, surpassing strong baselines in both unsupervised and semi-supervised settings. Our code is available at https://github.com/floatSDSDS/LANID.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LANID, a framework for New Intent Discovery (NID) in task-oriented dialogue systems. LANID leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to guide the training of lightweight encoders using contrastive learning.  It employs two data sampling strategies based on unsupervised algorithms (K-Nearest Neighbors and DBSCAN) to select informative utterance pairs. An LLM then labels the relationships between these pairs, which are used to create a contrastive learning task for fine-tuning a smaller encoder with triplet loss. The goal is to improve the encoder's ability to distinguish between known and new intents while maintaining efficiency and privacy. The approach is evaluated on three NID datasets, demonstrating improvements over existing baselines in both unsupervised and semi-supervised settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using LLMs to guide and enhance smaller encoders for NID is moderately novel. While prior work has explored LLMs for various NLP tasks, including zero-shot intent recognition, the approach of *distilling* knowledge from LLMs into a smaller, more efficient encoder using a contrastive learning framework is a significant and practical contribution. The two unsupervised data sampling methods contribute to more efficient use of the LLM.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in addressing the practical challenges of deploying LLMs in real-world dialogue systems. Directly using LLMs can be computationally expensive, slow, and raise privacy concerns. LANID offers a way to leverage the semantic understanding capabilities of LLMs offline to improve the performance of smaller, more manageable encoders. The empirical results support the claim that LANID improves performance compared to existing approaches.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper addresses a relevant and important problem in task-oriented dialogue systems.
    *   The proposed LANID framework is well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   The use of contrastive learning and triplet loss is appropriate for the NID task.
    *   The two data sampling strategies are interesting and demonstrate an understanding of unsupervised learning techniques.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, using multiple datasets and comparing against strong baselines.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on an LLM for labeling introduces a potential bottleneck and dependence on the LLM's accuracy and cost. The paper could benefit from more discussion on the potential impact of LLM errors on the performance of the fine-tuned encoder.
    *   The choice of hyper-parameters for the data sampling strategies and the triplet loss function are not fully justified. More ablation studies could be included to demonstrate the sensitivity of the results to these parameters.
    *   While the paper demonstrates good performance, it is not clear whether the improvements are consistent across different types of new intents.  Some intents might be easier to discover than others, and it would be helpful to analyze the performance on different intent categories.
    *   The privacy concerns surrounding LLMs are mentioned, but are not explored in depth within the paper.

*   **Impact and Influence:** LANID has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and scalable NID systems. The framework provides a blueprint for leveraging LLMs to improve smaller encoders, which could be valuable for researchers and practitioners working on dialogue systems. The data sampling strategies could also be adapted and applied to other machine learning tasks.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a practical and effective approach for new intent discovery. While there are some limitations regarding reliance on LLMs and hyperparameter choices, the overall contribution is valuable and addresses a relevant problem. The use of contrastive learning and unsupervised data sampling strategies is well-motivated.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contribution to the field. The method has novelty, addresses a real problem and demonstrates good empirical performance. However, there is room for improvement regarding the depth of analysis on failure cases or how the method works best, sensitivity to hyperparameters, or further reductions in reliance on the LLM. It is a valuable step forward but does not completely revolutionize NID.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Short-video Propagation Influence Rating: A New Real-world Dataset and A New Large Graph Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23746v1)
- **Authors**: Dizhan Xue, Jing Cui, Shengsheng Qian, Chuanrui Hu, Changsheng Xu
- **Abstract**: Short-video platforms have gained immense popularity, captivating the interest of millions, if not billions, of users globally. Recently, researchers have highlighted the significance of analyzing the propagation of short-videos, which typically involves discovering commercial values, public opinions, user behaviors, etc. This paper proposes a new Short-video Propagation Influence Rating (SPIR) task and aims to promote SPIR from both the dataset and method perspectives. First, we propose a new Cross-platform Short-Video (XS-Video) dataset, which aims to provide a large-scale and real-world short-video propagation network across various platforms to facilitate the research on short-video propagation. Our XS-Video dataset includes 117,720 videos, 381,926 samples, and 535 topics across 5 biggest Chinese platforms, annotated with the propagation influence from level 0 to 9. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first large-scale short-video dataset that contains cross-platform data or provides all of the views, likes, shares, collects, fans, comments, and comment content. Second, we propose a Large Graph Model (LGM) named NetGPT, based on a novel three-stage training mechanism, to bridge heterogeneous graph-structured data with the powerful reasoning ability and knowledge of Large Language Models (LLMs). Our NetGPT can comprehend and analyze the short-video propagation graph, enabling it to predict the long-term propagation influence of short-videos. Comprehensive experimental results evaluated by both classification and regression metrics on our XS-Video dataset indicate the superiority of our method for SPIR.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize the paper and provide a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new task, Short-video Propagation Influence Rating (SPIR), which aims to predict the long-term influence of a newly posted short video within a larger propagation network.  To facilitate research on this task, the authors present a large-scale, real-world cross-platform short-video dataset called XS-Video, containing data from the five biggest Chinese short-video platforms. The dataset includes rich information such as video content, post details, interactive metrics (views, likes, shares, etc.), and comment content.  They also construct a huge propagation graph based on the dataset. To address the SPIR task, the authors propose a novel Large Graph Model (LGM) named NetGPT, designed to bridge heterogeneous graph data with large language models (LLMs) via a three-stage training mechanism. Experimental results on the XS-Video dataset demonstrate the superiority of the proposed NetGPT model over state-of-the-art baselines, including GNNs, LLMs, and multimodal LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty on multiple fronts:

    *   **Task Definition:** The SPIR task is a novel contribution. While popularity prediction exists, SPIR aims for a more comprehensive, long-term, multi-dimensional influence rating, moving beyond single-indicator, short-term forecasts.
    *   **Dataset:** The XS-Video dataset is a significant contribution. Its scale, cross-platform nature, and the richness of its features (interactive metrics, comment content, cross-platform coverage) make it a valuable resource for the community. The construction of the large propagation graph from this dataset is also noteworthy. While other datasets exist, the scope and depth of XS-Video appear to be more comprehensive. The real-world nature and size of the data are particularly important.
    *   **Method:** The proposed NetGPT model presents a novel architecture to leverage LLMs in combination with heterogeneous graphs. The three-stage training mechanism (heterogeneous graph pre-training, supervised language fine-tuning, and task-oriented predictor fine-tuning) is a reasoned approach to bridge the gap between graph-structured data and LLMs.

*   **Significance:** The work has potential significance for:

    *   **Short-video Analysis Research:** The task and dataset could spur new research directions in short-video propagation, influence analysis, and related areas.
    *   **Practical Applications:** Accurate SPIR models could benefit various applications on short-video platforms, including advertising, recommendation, content moderation, and more.
    *   **Bridging LLMs and Graph Data:** The NetGPT model offers a promising approach to integrate LLMs with heterogeneous graph data, which could have implications beyond the specific task addressed in the paper.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive dataset and task definition.
    *   Well-motivated model architecture (NetGPT).
    *   Demonstrated empirical results.
    *   Clear and well-structured presentation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** While the dataset is cross-platform, it's limited to Chinese platforms. This potentially limits the generalizability of the findings and the applicability of the model to other cultural contexts or platform ecosystems.
    *   **Dependency on LLMs:** NetGPT's performance is closely tied to the LLM backbone. Improvements in LLMs could further enhance the model, but conversely, limitations in the LLM may constrain NetGPT's capabilities.
    *   **Incremental Improvement over LLMs:** While NetGPT improves on LLMs, the percentage difference may not always be drastic, indicating that LLMs still possess significant capabilities in this area even without explicit integration with graph structure. The ablation studies do, however, support the value of the specific approach.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Limitations** A discussion of known shortcomings of the proposed NetGPT architecture would strengthen the paper. Are there known biases that the model might have, or specific contexts where it fails to perform well? This will enhance transparency and promote further research in the area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper delivers a novel task, a valuable dataset, and a reasonable solution architecture with solid empirical evaluation. While some limitations exist, the overall contribution is substantial. The work addresses a relevant problem and offers a direction for future research. The paper will be useful for anyone working on short-video analysis.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### THEMIS: Towards Practical Intellectual Property Protection for Post-Deployment On-Device Deep Learning Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23748v1)
- **Authors**: Yujin Huang, Zhi Zhang, Qingchuan Zhao, Xingliang Yuan, Chunyang Chen
- **Abstract**: On-device deep learning (DL) has rapidly gained adoption in mobile apps, offering the benefits of offline model inference and user privacy preservation over cloud-based approaches. However, it inevitably stores models on user devices, introducing new vulnerabilities, particularly model-stealing attacks and intellectual property infringement. While system-level protections like Trusted Execution Environments (TEEs) provide a robust solution, practical challenges remain in achieving scalable on-device DL model protection, including complexities in supporting third-party models and limited adoption in current mobile solutions. Advancements in TEE-enabled hardware, such as NVIDIA's GPU-based TEEs, may address these obstacles in the future. Currently, watermarking serves as a common defense against model theft but also faces challenges here as many mobile app developers lack corresponding machine learning expertise and the inherent read-only and inference-only nature of on-device DL models prevents third parties like app stores from implementing existing watermarking techniques in post-deployment models. To protect the intellectual property of on-device DL models, in this paper, we propose THEMIS, an automatic tool that lifts the read-only restriction of on-device DL models by reconstructing their writable counterparts and leverages the untrainable nature of on-device DL models to solve watermark parameters and protect the model owner's intellectual property. Extensive experimental results across various datasets and model structures show the superiority of THEMIS in terms of different metrics. Further, an empirical investigation of 403 real-world DL mobile apps from Google Play is performed with a success rate of 81.14%, showing the practicality of THEMIS.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents THEMIS, an automatic tool designed to protect the intellectual property of on-device deep learning (DL) models against model-stealing attacks. The tool addresses the challenge that on-device models are typically read-only and inference-only, making traditional watermarking techniques difficult to apply. THEMIS works by reconstructing writable counterparts of these models, leveraging the non-trainable nature of on-device DL models to solve for watermark parameters, and then embedding these watermarks. The tool consists of four key steps: model extraction, model rooting (creating a writable model), model reweighting (embedding the watermark in a training-free manner), and DL app reassembling.  The paper evaluates THEMIS on several datasets and model architectures and performs an empirical investigation on real-world DL apps from Google Play.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of enabling watermarking in post-deployment on-device DL models by reconstructing writable counterparts is relatively novel. The paper addresses a specific and timely problem driven by the increasing adoption of on-device DL and the inherent vulnerabilities of storing models on user devices. While individual components (model extraction, data synthesis, backdoor algorithms) are not entirely novel, their integration within THEMIS to solve this particular problem offers a new contribution. The technique of "Model Rooting" to reconstruct a writable model is also a significant contribution, considering the read-only nature of on-device DL models.
* **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a real-world security and intellectual property concern. Model-stealing attacks are a growing threat, and THEMIS provides a practical approach to mitigate these risks for mobile app developers who may lack the expertise or resources for implementing traditional security measures. The evaluation on real-world apps increases the practical value of the research. The tool attempts to bridge the gap between theoretical attacks and practical defenses. The development of training-free watermarking (FFKEW) is also very significant, reducing the dependence on iterative optimization.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical Solution:** THEMIS provides an automated and practical solution that can be potentially adopted by app stores to protect on-device DL models.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The extensive evaluation across various datasets, model structures, and real-world apps provides strong evidence for the effectiveness and practicality of the tool.
    * **Addresses a Specific Challenge:** The paper explicitly tackles the constraints imposed by the read-only and inference-only nature of on-device models.
    * **Automated:** The tool is mostly automated, so it doesn't require specialized ML expertise for its use.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on App Store Control:**  The threat model assumes that the app store administrator has the necessary permissions and access to the app's internals for modification. This may not always be a realistic assumption. Broader application to different app ecosystems would strengthen the overall contribution.
    * **Overhead:** While not discussed in great detail, the reconstruction of writable models, re-assembly of apps, and watermarking processes likely incur some performance overhead that needs to be further investigated. The paper includes some information regarding total time, model extraction time, etc., which is good but more analysis of the overhead incurred on the actual mobile device is needed.
    * **Limited Robustness Discussion:** The robustness analysis focuses on model extraction attacks. There needs to be greater discussion of robustness against watermark removal attacks, especially given that the models are now writable. Even if the authors implement state-of-the-art training-free backdoor algorithms into Model Reweighting for increased robustness, these still need to be tested in evaluation.
    * **Label-Free Data Construction for All Scenarios**: The paper uses the DiffusoinDB for generating watermarked data when the label information is missing. There must be discussion about potential biases in the generated dataset and what impact those biases can have on the performance of the attack.
* **Potential Influence:**  THEMIS has the potential to influence the development of more robust security mechanisms for on-device AI. It could also encourage further research into training-free watermarking techniques and motivate the development of standards and best practices for securing DL models in mobile applications.  The emphasis on automation could make model protection more accessible to a wider range of developers.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the paper's novelty, practical significance, thorough evaluation, and weaknesses, a score of 7 is appropriate. The paper addresses a relevant and timely problem with a relatively novel and practical solution. The comprehensive evaluation adds to the paper's credibility. However, the limitations regarding its reliance on app store control, limited robustness analysis, and lack of detail regarding the performance overhead prevent it from achieving a higher score. Future work addressing these limitations could significantly enhance the impact and value of the research.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### StrokeFusion: Vector Sketch Generation via Joint Stroke-UDF Encoding and Latent Sequence Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23752v1)
- **Authors**: Jin Zhou, Yi Zhou, Pengfei Xu, Hui Huang
- **Abstract**: In the field of sketch generation, raster-format trained models often produce non-stroke artifacts, while vector-format trained models typically lack a holistic understanding of sketches, leading to compromised recognizability. Moreover, existing methods struggle to extract common features from similar elements (e.g., eyes of animals) appearing at varying positions across sketches. To address these challenges, we propose StrokeFusion, a two-stage framework for vector sketch generation. It contains a dual-modal sketch feature learning network that maps strokes into a high-quality latent space. This network decomposes sketches into normalized strokes and jointly encodes stroke sequences with Unsigned Distance Function (UDF) maps, representing sketches as sets of stroke feature vectors. Building upon this representation, our framework exploits a stroke-level latent diffusion model that simultaneously adjusts stroke position, scale, and trajectory during generation. This enables high-fidelity sketch generation while supporting stroke interpolation editing. Extensive experiments on the QuickDraw dataset demonstrate that our framework outperforms state-of-the-art techniques, validating its effectiveness in preserving structural integrity and semantic features. Code and models will be made publicly available upon publication.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces StrokeFusion, a novel two-stage framework for generating vector sketches.  The first stage employs a dual-modal encoder that learns sketch features by jointly encoding vector strokes and Unsigned Distance Function (UDF) maps. This produces disentangled stroke embeddings with explicit position and scale parameters. The second stage utilizes a stroke-level latent diffusion model to generate strokes in an unordered, non-autoregressive manner, predicting position, scale, and trajectory simultaneously using the stroke embeddings. The model allows for stroke interpolation and editing and demonstrates improved performance on the QuickDraw dataset compared to existing methods, preserving structural integrity and semantic features.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dual-Modal Encoding:** The core strength lies in the fusion of vector strokes and UDF maps. This addresses a key limitation of prior work by combining the advantages of both representations: geometric precision of vector graphics with the structural awareness of raster images. This is a well-motivated design choice.
    *   **Disentangled Learning:** The disentanglement of stroke position, scale, and shape contributes to the model's ability to extract common patterns across sketches. This addresses a significant challenge in sketch generation.
    *   **Non-Autoregressive Generation:** The use of a diffusion model for unordered stroke generation is a significant departure from traditional autoregressive methods, addressing the inherent ambiguity in stroke order. This enables more flexible sketch generation.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides convincing quantitative and qualitative results, demonstrating the superiority of StrokeFusion over state-of-the-art baselines on the QuickDraw dataset. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the framework.
    *   **Potential for Editing and Interpolation:** The structure of the model promotes ease of editing and interpolations on strokes.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Dependence:** The experiments are primarily conducted on the QuickDraw dataset, which is relatively simple. The generalizability of StrokeFusion to more complex and diverse sketch datasets requires further investigation. It should be noted that the QuickDraw dataset is still relatively expansive, but could limit generalizability.
    *   **Complexity of Implementation:** The dual-modal encoder and diffusion model introduce significant complexity. While the paper clearly outlines the architecture and training process, the computational cost and memory footprint of StrokeFusion might be higher than simpler methods.
    *   **Implicit Semantic Strokes**: The generation of a sketch can require an understanding of an object, as a composition of various strokes. However, in certain cases, the generated sketches require segmentation of strokes by hand in order to edit certain strokes.
    *   **Limited Ablation study**: the paper could improve via more in-depth and extensive ablation studies with the variation of decay coefficients.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper introduces a novel approach to vector sketch generation that combines the strengths of vector and raster representations.
    *   The dual-modal encoding and non-autoregressive generation strategy are significant contributions to the field.
    *   The results demonstrate a clear improvement in sketch quality compared to existing methods.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   StrokeFusion has the potential to advance the state-of-the-art in sketch generation, enabling more realistic and editable sketches.
    *   The framework can be applied to a wide range of applications, including design, animation, and interactive prototyping.
    *   The dual-modal encoding and non-autoregressive generation strategy can inspire new research directions in sketch generation and related fields.

**Justification of Score:**

StrokeFusion demonstrates clear novelty by combining vector and raster representations and utilizing a diffusion model for non-autoregressive stroke generation. The experimental results are compelling and the ablation studies provide valuable insights. The significance is also reasonably high, but is restricted by the dataset utilized. Overall, it is a significant contribution to the field of sketch generation.

### Time-Series Forecasting via Topological Information Supervised Framework with Efficient Topological Feature Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23757v1)
- **Authors**: ZiXin Lin, Nur Fariha Syaqina Zulkepli
- **Abstract**: Topological Data Analysis (TDA) has emerged as a powerful tool for extracting meaningful features from complex data structures, driving significant advancements in fields such as neuroscience, biology, machine learning, and financial modeling. Despite its success, the integration of TDA with time-series prediction remains underexplored due to three primary challenges: the limited utilization of temporal dependencies within topological features, computational bottlenecks associated with persistent homology, and the deterministic nature of TDA pipelines restricting generalized feature learning. This study addresses these challenges by proposing the Topological Information Supervised (TIS) Prediction framework, which leverages neural networks and Conditional Generative Adversarial Networks (CGANs) to generate synthetic topological features, preserving their distribution while significantly reducing computational time. We propose a novel training strategy that integrates topological consistency loss to improve the predictive accuracy of deep learning models. Specifically, we introduce two state-of-the-art models, TIS-BiGRU and TIS-Informer, designed to capture short-term and long-term temporal dependencies, respectively. Comparative experimental results demonstrate the superior performance of TIS models over conventional predictors, validating the effectiveness of integrating topological information. This work not only advances TDA-based time-series prediction but also opens new avenues for utilizing topological features in deep learning architectures.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper proposes a Topological Information Supervised (TIS) Prediction framework to enhance time-series forecasting by integrating topological data analysis (TDA). The framework addresses key challenges: limited use of temporal dependencies within topological features, high computational cost of persistent homology, and deterministic nature of TDA. It uses neural networks and conditional GANs (CGANs) to generate synthetic topological features, reducing computational time and allowing for more flexible feature learning. The framework includes a novel training strategy with a topological consistency loss. Two state-of-the-art models, TIS-BiGRU and TIS-Informer, are presented to capture short-term and long-term dependencies, respectively, and are experimentally shown to outperform conventional predictors.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Positive:** The key novelty lies in the integration of GAN-generated synthetic topological features within a supervised learning framework for time-series forecasting. It is also innovative to use these extracted topological features as regularization. This addresses limitations in traditional TDA applications within time-series prediction (computational bottlenecks, deterministic nature).
    *   **Neutral:** Some of the techniques used (BiGRU, Informer, GANs) are established methods in their respective fields. However, their combination and application in this specific context (TDA-enhanced time-series forecasting) are novel.
    *   **Negative:** The architectural choices and GAN configurations, while effective, may not be drastically different from existing implementations.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Positive:** Overcoming computational bottlenecks in TDA-based time-series forecasting has the potential to broaden the applicability of TDA to larger datasets and real-time scenarios. Demonstrating improved performance over conventional predictors validates the usefulness of integrating topological information. Also, the use of TDA as a regularization method for time-series is interesting.
    *   **Negative:** The significance is somewhat limited by the scope of the experiments. The datasets are relatively standard, and the improvements, while present, are not overwhelming. Also, the ablation study is limited. A more in-depth exploration of hyperparameter sensitivity and a wider range of time-series datasets would strengthen the claims. The study of the computational bottleneck is valuable but could be enhanced by looking at the wall-clock computation time.
    *   **Potential Impact:** The approach can potentially influence research on combining geometric and topological data analysis (GTDA) with deep learning for time-series forecasting and other sequence modeling tasks, especially when the computational cost of GTDA is high. The work could also inspire exploration of other generative models for creating diverse and informative topological feature representations.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a clear gap in the literature by integrating TDA with time-series prediction while tackling key limitations.
    *   Presents a well-defined and implemented framework with clearly described models (TIS-BiGRU, TIS-Informer).
    *   Includes experimental results supporting the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   Addresses the computational complexity in detail.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The experimental evaluation could be more extensive, including a larger variety of time-series datasets and a more thorough ablation study (e.g., varying GAN architectures, topological loss weighting).
    *   The architectural choices and GAN configurations, while effective, may not be drastically different from existing implementations.
    *   The interpretation of the specific topological features learned and their relationship to the time-series dynamics is limited. More insight into *why* the TIS models outperform conventional approaches would be valuable.
    *   The use of L2 loss is not the best for all applications.

*   **Clarity:**
    The paper is generally well-written and clearly explains the proposed framework and experimental setup.
    The method could be explained with some more details (for example, what topology features are used exactly)

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by effectively combining TDA with time-series forecasting using a GAN-supervised framework. The approach addresses critical limitations of applying TDA to this domain, particularly regarding computational cost and feature generalization. While the novelty of individual components (GANs, BiGRU, Informer) may be moderate, their integration within the TIS framework, along with the topological consistency loss, demonstrates innovation. While the evaluation is sound, a more comprehensive experimental analysis would further strengthen the paper. Therefore,

### STI-Bench: Are MLLMs Ready for Precise Spatial-Temporal World Understanding?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23765v1)
- **Authors**: Yun Li, Yiming Zhang, Tao Lin, XiangRui Liu, Wenxiao Cai, Zheng Liu, Bo Zhao
- **Abstract**: The use of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) as an end-to-end solution for Embodied AI and Autonomous Driving has become a prevailing trend. While MLLMs have been extensively studied for visual semantic understanding tasks, their ability to perform precise and quantitative spatial-temporal understanding in real-world applications remains largely unexamined, leading to uncertain prospects. To evaluate models' Spatial-Temporal Intelligence, we introduce STI-Bench, a benchmark designed to evaluate MLLMs' spatial-temporal understanding through challenging tasks such as estimating and predicting the appearance, pose, displacement, and motion of objects. Our benchmark encompasses a wide range of robot and vehicle operations across desktop, indoor, and outdoor scenarios. The extensive experiments reveals that the state-of-the-art MLLMs still struggle in real-world spatial-temporal understanding, especially in tasks requiring precise distance estimation and motion analysis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces STI-Bench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the spatial-temporal reasoning abilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). STI-Bench uses video inputs across diverse settings (desktop, indoor, outdoor) and assesses models on tasks like object pose estimation, displacement calculation, trajectory description, and dimensional measurement. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art MLLMs and find that they still struggle with these tasks, especially those requiring precise distance estimation and motion analysis.  The paper identifies three main limitations of current MLLMs: inaccurate spatial quantification, flawed temporal dynamics understanding, and weak cross-modal grounding and integration. The authors argue that STI-Bench can help guide future development of MLLMs for embodied AI and autonomous driving.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the *specific* focus on spatial-temporal intelligence for MLLMs using *video* inputs. While existing benchmarks evaluate MLLMs' general capabilities and video understanding, STI-Bench directly targets the ability to reason about 3D space and time from videos.  The task definition and the dataset curation strategy tailored for spatial-temporal reasoning are also noteworthy.  It is a shift from primarily semantic tasks to a more physically grounded assessment.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation of current MLLMs.  Embodied AI and autonomous driving require more than just semantic understanding; they need precise spatial-temporal reasoning.  By demonstrating the shortcomings of current models on these tasks, the paper highlights a key area for future research. The benchmark provides the research community with a valuable tool to objectively evaluate and improve MLLMs' abilities in this area. This, in turn, is critical for making progress on real-world applications.  The error analysis offers useful insights into the root causes of the models' failures.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined tasks covering different aspects of spatial-temporal reasoning.
    *   Diverse dataset covering a range of scenarios.
    *   Clear evaluation metrics.
    *   Thorough analysis of model performance, including error analysis.
    *   Comparison of various MLLM architectures (API-based and Open-source)
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the scenarios are diverse, the number of QA pairs and videos (around 2000 and 300) is relatively small compared to some other MLLM benchmarks. This could limit the generalizability of the findings.
    *   The paper focuses primarily on multiple-choice question answering. While this provides a quantitative measure of performance, it might not fully capture the nuances of spatial-temporal reasoning. More open-ended evaluation could be beneficial.
    *   The benchmark curation relies on existing datasets. The paper would be strengthened by a detailed analysis of potential biases inherited from these source datasets.
    *   The paper does not explore methods to improve MLLM performance on the benchmark, which could have added to its impact. It focuses on evaluating rather than mitigating current limitations.
    *   It is important to note that the models are not finetuned to improve the spatial temporal capabilites on STI-Bench.
*   **Potential Influence:** STI-Bench has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating spatial-temporal intelligence in MLLMs.  It could drive research towards addressing the identified limitations and developing more capable MLLMs for embodied AI and autonomous driving. The insights into error patterns can also inform the design of better training strategies and architectures. However, its influence will depend on its adoption by the research community.

Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution by highlighting a critical gap in MLLM capabilities and providing a benchmark for future progress. While there is room for improvement in terms of dataset size and evaluation methods, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. It definitely addresses a real problem by providing the benchmark, and can be helpful for researchers.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The score reflects the paper's clear contribution to the field and its potential influence. While it has some limitations (smaller dataset, limited exploration of solutions), it provides a valuable resource for evaluating a crucial aspect of MLLM performance. The identified weaknesses are addressable in future work, building upon the foundation laid by this paper.  The novelty is reasonably high, and the significance is tied directly to real-world applications where spatial-temporal reasoning is essential. The score is less than 8 because other aspects of benchmarking like bias and scaling are not explored to their fullest potential. A rigorous finetuning experiment would have increased the score as well.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Biologically Inspired Spiking Diffusion Model with Adaptive Lateral Selection Mechanism
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23767v1)
- **Authors**: Linghao Feng, Dongcheng Zhao, Sicheng Shen, Yi Zeng
- **Abstract**: Lateral connection is a fundamental feature of biological neural circuits, facilitating local information processing and adaptive learning. In this work, we integrate lateral connections with a substructure selection network to develop a novel diffusion model based on spiking neural networks (SNNs). Unlike conventional artificial neural networks, SNNs employ an intrinsic spiking inner loop to process sequential binary spikes. We leverage this spiking inner loop alongside a lateral connection mechanism to iteratively refine the substructure selection network, enhancing model adaptability and expressivity. Specifically, we design a lateral connection framework comprising a learnable lateral matrix and a lateral mapping function, both implemented using spiking neurons, to dynamically update lateral connections. Through mathematical modeling, we establish that the proposed lateral update mechanism, under a well-defined local objective, aligns with biologically plausible synaptic plasticity principles. Extensive experiments validate the effectiveness of our approach, analyzing the role of substructure selection and lateral connection during training. Furthermore, quantitative comparisons demonstrate that our model consistently surpasses state-of-the-art SNN-based generative models across multiple benchmark datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a biologically inspired spiking diffusion model enhanced with an adaptive lateral selection mechanism. It integrates lateral connections, a feature of biological neural circuits, with a substructure selection network within a spiking neural network (SNN) diffusion model. The key innovation lies in using the spiking inner loop of SNNs, combined with a learned lateral connection framework (learnable lateral matrix and mapping function implemented using spiking neurons), to iteratively refine the substructure selection network. The authors theoretically demonstrate that the lateral update mechanism aligns with synaptic plasticity principles like STDP and empirically validate their approach by showing superior performance compared to existing SNN-based generative models on several benchmark datasets. The ablation studies confirm the importance of both the substructure selection and lateral connection mechanisms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of lateral connections and a substructure selection network within an SNN-based diffusion model is a noteworthy contribution. While SNNs and diffusion models exist individually, their combination with a biologically plausible lateral connection mechanism is relatively novel. The theoretical justification for the lateral update mechanism aligning with STDP principles adds to the paper's novelty. The use of a transformer architecture in the context of spiking neural networks also adds to the paper's incremental novelty. However, the novelty is not a complete paradigm shift, but rather a well-engineered integration of existing concepts.
*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates promising results on several benchmark datasets, surpassing existing SNN-based generative models. This suggests that the proposed approach could advance the development of more efficient and biologically plausible generative models. The emphasis on energy-efficient computation and the potential for deployment on neuromorphic hardware are significant. The analysis of the roles of substructure selection and lateral connections during training offers valuable insights for future research in this area. However, the significance is limited by the fact that SNNs, although promising, are not yet widely adopted in all machine learning applications, and their advantages depend heavily on specialized hardware.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong empirical results on multiple datasets.
    *   Theoretical justification grounded in biological plausibility (STDP).
    *   Clear and well-structured presentation.
    *   Comprehensive ablation studies.
    *   In-depth analysis of the dynamics of the selection mechanism and lateral parameters.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The biological plausibility argument, while present, could be further strengthened with more explicit links to biological data/experiments.
    *   The performance gains, while consistent, are not always drastically better than existing methods, especially in some scenarios.
    *   The computational complexity might be high. Specific numbers or analyses of the model's computational cost in relation to its performance gains would be helpful.
    *   The reliance on surrogate gradients, a standard technique for training SNNs, limits the direct biological accuracy compared to more biologically realistic, but harder to train, methods.
    *   The paper focuses primarily on image generation. Expanding the evaluation to other generative tasks or modalities would broaden the impact.

**Overall Score:**

Given the novelty and significance of the presented work. I would assign the following score:

Score: 7

**Rationale:** The paper presents a meaningful contribution to the field of SNNs and generative modeling. The combination of lateral connections and substructure selection is a novel approach with solid experimental support. The theoretical link to STDP strengthens the work's credibility. However, while the empirical results are positive, the improvements are incremental, and the reliance on surrogate gradients and specialized hardware limits the direct applicability and real-world impact of SNNs for machine learning. A score of 7 reflects the value of the contribution while acknowledging the limitations. The paper advances the understanding and capabilities of SNNs, but further research is needed to fully realize their potential in practical applications.

- **Classification**: cs.NE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Texture or Semantics? Vision-Language Models Get Lost in Font Recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23768v1)
- **Authors**: Zhecheng Li, Guoxian Song, Yujun Cai, Zhen Xiong, Junsong Yuan, Yiwei Wang
- **Abstract**: Modern Vision-Language Models (VLMs) exhibit remarkable visual and linguistic capabilities, achieving impressive performance in various tasks such as image recognition and object localization. However, their effectiveness in fine-grained tasks remains an open question. In everyday scenarios, individuals encountering design materials, such as magazines, typography tutorials, research papers, or branding content, may wish to identify aesthetically pleasing fonts used in the text. Given their multimodal capabilities and free accessibility, many VLMs are often considered potential tools for font recognition. This raises a fundamental question: Do VLMs truly possess the capability to recognize fonts? To investigate this, we introduce the Font Recognition Benchmark (FRB), a compact and well-structured dataset comprising 15 commonly used fonts. FRB includes two versions: (i) an easy version, where 10 sentences are rendered in different fonts, and (ii) a hard version, where each text sample consists of the names of the 15 fonts themselves, introducing a stroop effect that challenges model perception. Through extensive evaluation of various VLMs on font recognition tasks, we arrive at the following key findings: (i) Current VLMs exhibit limited font recognition capabilities, with many state-of-the-art models failing to achieve satisfactory performance. (ii) Few-shot learning and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting provide minimal benefits in improving font recognition accuracy across different VLMs. (iii) Attention analysis sheds light on the inherent limitations of VLMs in capturing semantic features.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Texture or Semantics? Vision-Language Models Get Lost in Font Recognition" investigates the ability of Vision-Language Models (VLMs) to perform fine-grained font recognition.  The authors introduce a new Font Recognition Benchmark (FRB) dataset comprising 15 common fonts, with both an "easy" version (sentences in different fonts) and a "hard" version (font names displayed in mismatched fonts, creating a Stroop effect).  Through extensive experiments on 13 VLMs (both open-source and closed-source), they find that VLMs struggle with font recognition, are susceptible to the Stroop effect, and do not significantly benefit from few-shot learning or Chain-of-Thought prompting.  Attention analysis suggests that the models fail to focus on critical font-defining features, instead being overly influenced by texture and textual content.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its focused examination of a specific fine-grained task (font recognition) using VLMs and introducing a new benchmark tailored to highlight specific challenges. While VLMs have been tested on numerous tasks, this deep dive into font recognition is less explored. The Stroop effect hard version of the benchmark is also a novel approach to assess model robustness against cognitive biases.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in highlighting the limitations of current VLMs in a seemingly simple, yet fine-grained visual task. VLMs have made remarkable progress in many areas; however, this paper underscores the point that they still lack the nuanced understanding and attention to detail required for tasks that humans find intuitive. This understanding is important for guiding future research on improving VLM capabilities in specific areas such as typography. The benchmark itself provides a valuable resource for future VLM evaluations.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined Problem:** Clearly articulates the research question and its relevance.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation includes a reasonably large set of VLMs, including both proprietary and open-source options.
    *   **Novel Dataset:** The FRB dataset is well-motivated and designed to expose specific VLM weaknesses, particularly the Stroop effect version.
    *   **In-depth Analysis:** The inclusion of attention map visualizations provides insights into why VLMs fail, going beyond simple accuracy metrics.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide code and datasets on GitHub, promoting reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Font Diversity:** While 15 fonts represent a starting point, the benchmark would be strengthened by expanding the font range to incorporate more complex and/or less common typefaces.
    *   **Inference Limitations:** The paper acknowledges computational limits preventing few-shot inference of stronger closed-source models. It weakens findings related to few-shot learning.
    *   **Prompt Engineering:** The paper acknowledges the simplicity of prompts used and suggests potential benefits from advanced prompt engineering. There is a concern that performance may be limited by prompt engineering or the inability of models to take complex prompts.
    *   **Lack of Specific Improvement Strategies:** While the paper identifies the problem, it does not propose concrete architectures or training strategies for improving VLM font recognition performance.

*   **Impact:** The paper is likely to have a moderate impact. It serves as a cautionary tale about the current limitations of VLMs and provides a benchmark for evaluating future models. It could inspire research into attention mechanisms, fine-grained feature extraction, or task-specific training approaches for VLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **6**.

*   The paper identifies a clear gap in VLM capabilities and presents a well-designed benchmark for evaluating font recognition.
*   The analysis is thorough and includes useful insights from attention visualizations.
*   The limitations regarding font diversity, few-shot evaluation on top-tier models, and the absence of solutions to address the identified problem prevent a higher score.

The work makes a solid contribution by highlighting specific VLM weaknesses and providing a foundation for future research in this area. However, the score is limited by the weaknesses mentioned above.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### XLRS-Bench: Could Your Multimodal LLMs Understand Extremely Large Ultra-High-Resolution Remote Sensing Imagery?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23771v1)
- **Authors**: Fengxiang Wang, Hongzhen Wang, Mingshuo Chen, Di Wang, Yulin Wang, Zonghao Guo, Qiang Ma, Long Lan, Wenjing Yang, Jing Zhang, Zhiyuan Liu, Maosong Sun
- **Abstract**: The astonishing breakthrough of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) has necessitated new benchmarks to quantitatively assess their capabilities, reveal their limitations, and indicate future research directions. However, this is challenging in the context of remote sensing (RS), since the imagery features ultra-high resolution that incorporates extremely complex semantic relationships. Existing benchmarks usually adopt notably smaller image sizes than real-world RS scenarios, suffer from limited annotation quality, and consider insufficient dimensions of evaluation. To address these issues, we present XLRS-Bench: a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating the perception and reasoning capabilities of MLLMs in ultra-high-resolution RS scenarios. XLRS-Bench boasts the largest average image size (8500$\times$8500) observed thus far, with all evaluation samples meticulously annotated manually, assisted by a novel semi-automatic captioner on ultra-high-resolution RS images. On top of the XLRS-Bench, 16 sub-tasks are defined to evaluate MLLMs' 10 kinds of perceptual capabilities and 6 kinds of reasoning capabilities, with a primary emphasis on advanced cognitive processes that facilitate real-world decision-making and the capture of spatiotemporal changes. The results of both general and RS-focused MLLMs on XLRS-Bench indicate that further efforts are needed for real-world RS applications. We have open-sourced XLRS-Bench to support further research in developing more powerful MLLMs for remote sensing.
